# Sprint 25 Hardening Construction Plan

**Status:** Adopted · **Last updated:** 2026-11-13

This plan breaks [Sprint Plan](01.%20Sprint%20Plan.md) §5's Sprint 25 into the order its rows run in. It sits **one level below** the sprint plan and does not change its entry or exit criteria. Where the two disagree, the sprint plan wins.

It is the seventeenth construction plan, after [20](20.%20Sprint%2024%20Builder%20Pair%20Close%20Construction%20Plan.md), and **it plans the first of the two hardening sprints**, Sprints 25–26, with `v1.0.0` at Sprint 26. It answers [status report 25](../status/25.%20Sprint%2024%20Status.md) (*Next Sprint Entry Criteria* 2–8 and *Blockers*) and [retrospective 22](../retrospectives/22.%20Sprint%2024%20Retrospective.md)'s five actions. **It sizes hardening's own work for the first time**: the load test, the scalability checks and the security pass, encryption at rest included, which the [sprint plan](01.%20Sprint%20Plan.md) §6 and the [Product Backlog](02.%20Product%20Backlog.md) §4's NFR table put in Sprint 25. It counts that work with the eighteen-issue tail, [#699](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/699) among them, and the 24 issues Sprint 24 filed, [#773](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/773) and [#774](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/774) among them.

**It raises one decision, D-118** (§3.3, §8): the count does not show that Sprints 25–26 hold everything placed in hardening. **The table above the line is the same under every answer.** **Dated note, 2026-10-26: the product owner answered D-118 the same day with C, against this plan's recommendation of A.** Sprint 26 takes the rows below Sprint 25's line that have not merged by Sprint 25's close, and lands them before its freeze; `v1.0.0` stays at Sprint 26 ([Product Backlog](02.%20Product%20Backlog.md) §6.1; §3.3 and §8 here). **It records one answer that takes no number**: the product owner approved [#721](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/721)'s ceiling on 2026-10-26, **80 states or 240 transitions, whichever comes first**. Above it the graph declines, says so, and offers the list. It is row 5, 300–600 lines.

## 1. Three things this plan says before anything else

### 1.1 Hardening is sized, and the two sprints do not hold all of it

Sprint 24, the last sprint of the same shape (one reading, no release), added 29,111 lines. **At Sprint 24's measured ratios, Sprint 25 comes to about 36,300–38,500**, which is 7,200–9,400 over 29,111 (§3.3). The line comes to about 20,700–21,200, at or just over Sprint 22's 20,714.

### 1.2 Record 24 gated six modules, and has landed

**Nothing has read Sprint 24** ([status report 25](../status/25.%20Sprint%2024%20Status.md), *Sprint 24, and who read it*). Its range, `59b42fe..a9f1cdf`, is closed: [#724](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/724) to [#772](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/772), 21 squash commits and 29,111 lines. **Record 24's reader has not started** (retrospective 22, second action). **The product owner starts it on Sprint 25's first day at the latest, from §7.1's brief, and it can start now.** *(Landed 2026-10-27: see the dated note below.)*

**No Sprint 25 row that changes a module Sprint 24 changed merges before record 24 lands and its blocking findings are fixed.** Those modules are:
- **workflow**: `kelir-backend/src/modules/workflow/`, and `kelir-frontend/src/features/workflow-builder/`, where [#770](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/770) changed `jwssRegistry.ts`;
- **document-type**: `kelir-backend/src/modules/document_type/`, and `kelir-frontend/src/features/admin/DocumentTypeFormDialog.vue`;
- **auth**: `kelir-backend/src/modules/auth/`, with `identity::repository::role_codes_for_user`, which #770 changed;
- **rad and JFSS**: `kelir-backend/src/modules/rad/`, `kelir-frontend/src/features/rad/`, and JFSS and its registries under `docs/schema/`;
- **the admin form builder**: `kelir-frontend/src/features/admin/form-builder/`, with `kelir-frontend/src/components/logic-builder/`, which it mounts (taken in by this plan's call, not because Sprint 24 is known to have changed it);
- **CI and scripts**: `.github/workflows/` and `scripts/`.

## 3. Capacity, measured by the template's rule

**The measure is [the template](../status/00.%20Status%20Report%20Template.md)'s**: the added lines of the merged squash commit, less generated files, split into feature, tests and docs. Status report 25's method counts in-file `#[cfg(test)]` modules as feature.

| Sprint | Planned | Built | **Actual** | Estimates |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 22 | 7 above, the run last | 17 | **20,714** | 1.3–2.4×; tests 73% |
| 23 | 9 above, 4 below | 13 and row 6b | **34,877** | 1.2–2.6×; tests 62% |
| 24 | 9 above, 8 below | 17, with #728 and #739 | **29,111** | **0.82–1.65×**: above the line 0.76–1.51×, below it 0.99–2.04×; tests 69% ([status report 25](../status/25.%20Sprint%2024%20Status.md)) |
| **25** | **18 above, 13 below** | — | — | — |

**The line is set against Sprint 22's 20,714, and the rows below it run up to Sprint 24's 29,111.**
- **Sprint 25 carries one reading and no release**, as Sprints 23 and 24 did. Of those two, **Sprint 24 is the later and the lower**, and its sized rows summed inside their range. So it bounds what is reached below the line. Sprint 23's 34,877 is the outer bound, and it was the sprint whose estimates missed most.
- **The line stays at 20,714**, where plans 19 and 20 set it, because **rows 1 and 2 have no comparable** in the tree: nothing here has built a load-test harness or encryption at rest. Sprint 24's line, held to 20,714, came in at 19,697 with #728 and #739.

### 3.1 The rules, revised by Sprint 24's measurement (retrospective 22, fourth action)

**A row number in this subsection is plan 20's, unless it says plan 21.** Status report 25 compared each rule with Sprint 24's measured rows (*Estimate against actual*).

1. **The small-row range, 350–750, for a few lines riding on code that exists. Held**: plan 20's row 11 came in at 494 with 13 feature lines. Unchanged. It sizes plan 21's rows 10, 27 and #773's half of row 19. **A control sized for its probes is 450–800**, plan 19's gate-lane size. Plan 20 sized row 9 at 350–700, and it came in at 722; plan 19's row 9 came in at 407. The tail's controls were sized at 450–800 by plan 19 and keep it.
2. **Changed: a text row pinned by tests is sized 550–900**, between plan 20's row 8 at 591 and plan 18's row 16 at 855. Rule 2 priced row 8 as a campaign row, 686–1,300, and it came in 0.86× the bottom. **An unpinned text row is 40–200**: row 15 was sized 60–200 and came in at 44, of which 24 were its texts.
3. **A walk is sized against a comparable with a similar count of subjects. Unchanged**: it governed no Sprint 24 row. It governs plan 21's row 13, which counts its routes first.
4. **A page row is sized from measured totals. Held**: row 2 came in at 6,567, inside 4,840–10,110, with tests at 60%. Unchanged. No Sprint 25 row is a page row. Plan 21's row 22 is dialogs, sized by plan 19 against plan 18's row 9.
5. **A workflow-seam campaign row is 1,200–2,500. Widened**: rows 5 and 6 came in at 1,693 and 1,843, inside, and row 14 at 2,455, 1.23× its top. Row 12 came in at 1,369. The range runs from row 12 to row 14.
6. **New: a race row is 1,250–1,900.** A row that adds a lock, and whose campaign stages the race it closes, came in at 1,269 to 1,846 every time Sprint 24 built one: #739 at 1,269, #728 at 1,765, row 6 at 1,843 and row 10 at 1,846. It governs plan 21's rows 6, 7 and, under one option, 8.
7. **New: no row is padded for its campaign.** Rows 4, 10, 13 and 14, #728 and #739 came in at 1.20–1.41× their tops, each carried by its tests. **The sprint's sum still landed inside its range.** So a row's range stays its comparables', and the sum, the line's room and the mid-sprint trigger (§7 step 5) absorb the overruns. Totals come from comparables, never from a test share.

**Two rows have no comparable, and the table says so**: row 1, the load test, and row 2, encryption at rest. Each is sized by its columns, as plan 12 sized its item 2 without one. **Each is re-sized when its criteria land**, with the date.

**Status report 26 compares each row with these rules applied.**

### 3.3 Hardening's length, and D-118

**What Sprint 25 holds, by origin:**

| Part | Rows | **Lines** |
|---|---|---|
| Hardening's own work | 1–4 | ~~**2,400–5,300**~~ ~~**2,730–5,950**~~ **3,030–6,700**, unsized until now (re-sized 2026-10-27: rows 1 and 2, §3.2; and 2026-10-28: row 2, for **D-122**) |
| #721's remedy | 5 | 300–600 |
| The 24 issues Sprint 24 filed, #773 and #774 among them | 6–10, 16 (#730, #731), 19–21 (#749, #771), 23–30 | **11,950–24,900**: six `should` at 4,950–8,650, and eighteen `could` at 7,000–16,250 |
| The tail plan 19 placed, with #699 | 11–17 (#557 in row 16), 21 (#671), 22, 31 | **7,298–15,900**: plan 19's 7,218–15,100, #699's 80–300, and #645 bounded at 0–500 |
| The follow-up rule | 18 | 10–60 |
| **Sprint 25** | **1–31** | ~~**21,958–46,760**~~ ~~**22,288–47,410**~~ **22,588–48,160** (re-sized 2026-10-27, and 2026-10-28 for D-122) |

**Against what has been built:**
- **The bottom fits**: 7,153 under Sprint 24's 29,111.
- **The top does not**: 17,649 over 29,111, and 11,883 over Sprint 23's 34,877.
- **At Sprint 24's ratios the sprint is about 36,300–38,470**: ~~7,190–9,360~~ 7,189–9,359 over 29,111, and ~~1,430–3,590~~ 1,420–3,590 over 34,877 (corrected 2026-10-26: 36,300 less 34,877 is 1,423, and 36,300 less 29,111 is 7,189).
- **Below the line, at Sprint 24's below-the-line ratios (2.04× and 0.99×), the rows come to about 16,750–18,580.** The room under 29,111, after a line of about 20,720–21,210, is about 7,900–8,390. **So about 8,400–10,700 lines of rows 19–31 are expected not to merge in Sprint 25.** About half.

**Sprint 26 cannot take them as the plan stands.** Its outline is the restore drill, the auditability, usability and compatibility checks, documentation completion and the `v1.0.0` run. **None of it is sized**, and plan 22 sizes it. As an outline only: the drill ~~300–800~~ 500–1,300, with NFR-AVA-001 to 003 added to it, the auditability check 1,200–4,500 (a walk over every mutating route, rule 3), usability 400–1,500, compatibility 200–800 and documentation 500–2,000, with a check that every route is in the generated OpenAPI document (NFR-MNT-005, FR-API-004), so **about ~~2,600–9,600~~ 2,800–10,100 (2,900–10,500 with rows 1 and 4's rerun, §3.3's table) with the run, record 25 and a pre-tag reading beside it** *(dated note, 2026-10-26: widened by `requirements-analyst`'s impact note on D-118, §8)*. Sprint 22, the last release sprint, built 20,714. **The sprint plan §6 keeps the tail out of the sprint that tags** ("none of it is beside the `v1.0.0` run", plan 13 §1.2's rule).

**So Sprints 25–26 hold hardening's own work and every `should` issue with `v1.0.0` at Sprint 26, and not every `could` issue.** That is **D-118** (§8), raised here as status report 25's third entry criterion asks: the cut, a third hardening sprint, or Sprint 26 taking the `could` rows before its freeze. **The rows above the line are the same under every answer**, and so is their order below it. Only where an unmerged row 19–31 goes differs.

**Dated note, 2026-10-26: D-118 was answered C, so Sprint 26 takes the carry-over, and is counted again with it.** The sentence above that Sprint 26 *cannot take them as the plan stands* was true of the plan before the answer; C changes the plan. **The rule C narrows**: the sprint plan §6's *none of it is beside the `v1.0.0` run*, which is plan 13 §1.2's rule applied to hardening. **It is narrowed only for Sprint 25's rows below its line that have not merged by Sprint 25's close, and only before Sprint 26's freeze.** Every other tail item, and anything after the freeze, stays out of the run.

**Sprint 26, restated with the carry-over.** Estimates, for plan 22 to size properly:

| Part | Lines |
|---|---|
| Restore drill, with NFR-AVA-001 to 003 | 500–1,300 |
| Auditability walk over every mutating route | 1,200–4,500 |
| Usability check | 400–1,500 |
| Compatibility check | 200–800 |
| Documentation completion, with the OpenAPI coverage check | 500–2,000 |
| **Rows 1 and 4 run again on the release candidate** (C's cost: the carry-over lands after Sprint 25's measurement) | 100–400, the harnesses being built |
| **Sprint 26's own work** | **2,900–10,500**; at Sprint 24's whole-sprint ratios (1.65× and 0.82×), about **4,800–8,600** |
| **Carried from Sprint 25**, rows 19–31 not merged by its close, projected at Sprint 24's below-the-line ratios (§3.3 above) | **about 8,400–10,700** |
| **Sprint 26** | **about 13,200–19,300** at those ratios; 11,300–21,200 by the raw estimates |

**Does Sprint 26 still hold? By the same comparison, yes, narrowly.** Sprint 22, the last sprint that carried a release run and a pre-tag reading, built 20,714. Sprint 26's projection is 1,400–7,500 under it. At the raw estimates' top it is 490 over. **The room left at the top, about 1,400 lines, is one race row** (§3.1 rule 6, 1,250–1,900), and it is all the room record 25's and the pre-tag reading's blocking fixes have. Sprint 24's 29,111 was a sprint with no release, and is not the comparison here.

**What C costs, by `requirements-analyst`'s impact note (§8):**
- **Product changes enter the range record 25 and the pre-tag reading must read.** Each carried row is read before the version bump, as every Sprint 23–26 row is.
- **Rows 1 and 4 run again on the release candidate**, after the carried rows merge, so the NFR-PERF-001 and NFR-SCA-001 figures `v1.0.0` states are the candidate's.
- **Rows 22 and 19, which touch NFR-USE-005, land before Sprint 26's usability check**, or the check records them as known failures.
- **Sprint 26's room holds the carried rows beside its auditability walk**, whose top is 4,500.

**If Sprint 26 does not hold.** C's own fallback applies: **a carried row not merged by Sprint 26's freeze goes after `v1.0.0`**, and status report 27 and `v1.0.0`'s release notes name it as known and open, with the requirement it touches (§8's trace). That needs no new decision. **What the product owner would face next is only this**: holding the freeze or the tag for such a row, which is a third hardening sprint taken late, D-118's option B, and moves `v1.0.0` to Sprint 27. **That would be the next free number, which is ~~D-119 today~~ ~~D-120 since 2026-10-27, when the NFR trace took D-119 for NFR-SEC-010 (§8)~~ ~~D-123 since 2026-10-28~~ ~~D-125 since 2026-10-29~~ ~~D-137 since 2026-10-30~~ ~~D-138 since 2026-10-31~~ ~~D-139~~ ~~D-140~~ ~~D-141 since 2026-11-01~~ ~~D-142 since 2026-11-05~~ ~~D-143 since 2026-11-06~~ ~~D-147 since 2026-11-07~~ ~~D-148 since 2026-11-10~~ D-149 since 2026-11-10 (struck and restated 2026-10-31, three times, the second for D-137, §5's eighth state note, and the third for D-138, the ninth; 2026-11-01, for D-140; 2026-11-05, for D-141, the twenty-first; 2026-11-06, for D-142, raised and answered by row 29's criteria, the twenty-fifth; 2026-11-08, for D-143 to D-146, raised by [#916](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/916) on 2026-11-07, the twenty-seventh; 2026-11-10, for D-147, raised for [#941](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/941), the thirty-third; and later that day, for D-148, raised and answered for NFR-AVA-004, the thirty-fourth), the NFR trace having taken D-119 for NFR-SEC-010 on 2026-10-27, the product owner D-120 to D-122 on 2026-10-28 (§8; struck and restated that day), and D-123 and D-124 on 2026-10-29 (struck and restated 2026-10-29: D-123 and D-124 were raised and answered, Product Backlog §6.1), raised at Sprint 26's mid-sprint refinement if the count there shows it.** It is not owed now.

## 4. The items, in the order they run

| Order | Item | Issue | Why here |
|---|---|---|---|
| R24 | **Record 24: the reading of `59b42fe..a9f1cdf`.** A reading, not a row | — | **The product owner starts it as a top-level session, on Sprint 25's first day at the latest; ~~it can start now~~ it landed 2026-10-27 as [#792](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/792), with no blocking finding.** Brief in §7.1. **Its file name says `Sprint 24`.** It lands before any row that changes one of §1.2's six modules merges, and a session that is not its reader fixes its blocking findings first |
| T | **`requirements-analyst`'s NFR trace**, one pass over NFR-PERF-001 to 004, NFR-SCA-001 to 004 and NFR-SEC-001 to 010 | F1–F4, [#777](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/777)–[#780](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/780) | **Ahead of rows 1–4.** It says, per NFR, what is met with which named test, what is not, and what each row must prove. **For NFR-PERF-001 it pins what *list view* and *detail view* mean in requests**, the data set, the user count and the machine the target is read on. | 2 | **Encryption at rest: attachments and backups (NFR-SEC-009)** | F2, [#778](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/778) | **No configuration in `deploy/` encrypts either today**, by a search of the folder. **The ADR comes first**, by `doc-author` with the `write-adr` skill, in this plan and before the row is built ([standards/06](../../docs/standards/06.%20Architecture%20Decision%20Records.md) §1: expensive to reverse once objects are stored, and it trades operability for a requirement). It weighs the object store encrypting what it holds, the application encrypting before it stores, and an operator's encrypted volume. **This plan sizes the first.** **If the ADR prefers the third, that meets a `must` requirement by an operator's duty, which is a scope call, raised as the next `D-n` before the ADR is adopted.** `rust-backend` builds `deploy/` and the scripts; `doc-author` the Installation guide §8's backup and restore. **If the object store needs a key service in its own image, that image is pinned** (**D-62**). *(2026-10-27: the ADR is [ADR-0049](../../docs/architectures/adr/0049.%20The%20Object%20Store%20Encrypts%20Attachments%20Under%20Its%20Own%20Key,%20and%20a%20Backup%20Is%20Encrypted%20to%20a%20Key%20the%20Host%20Does%20Not%20Hold.md), `Draft` and blocked by #778 until this row's build flips it. It prefers the first option. MinIO encrypts with SSE-S3 under its built-in static key, so there is no key service and no new image. Archives are `pg_dump` piped through `age` to a recipient whose identity the host does not hold. **No `D-n` is raised.** Whether the backups should also take the object store, which the timer does not today, is NFR-AVA-004's question, and the ADR leaves it open.)* *(2026-10-28: **D-122** answers it yes (§8). Today the timer takes the database only (`provision-ubuntu-24.sh` §6, `pg_dump`). #778's *Done when*, [widened that day](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/778#issuecomment-6462911831), adds the bucket copied beside each dump, on the same timer, with the same retention and to the same `age` recipients (MinIO decrypts SSE-S3 on read, so the copy is encrypted by `age`, not by the store); the pre-deploy backup taking it too; a raw read of a backed-up object in the probe; and a restore of both halves, an attachment downloaded and matched, within NFR-AVA-004's RTO. **`rust-backend` builds the copy with the dump; `test-engineer` the probe and the restore check; `doc-author` the Installation guide §8, which says what a backup holds, and ADR-0049's record of D-122.** Re-sized to 1,200–2,750, ADR-0049's figure, §3.2. **ADR-0049 §2 moves the backup unit from `postgres` to root**, so that it can read MinIO's root pair from `.env` and reach the store through Docker.)* *(Merged 2026-10-28 as [#806](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/806), 1,524 added lines, closing #778, with ADR-0049 adopted in it. Its campaign found [#807](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/807), row 41; §5's third state note.)* |

| 47 *(added 2026-11-09)* | **The probe sees a restore that drops `--enc-s3`** | [#921](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/921) | Row 41's build found it, `could`, so below the line. **The probe's scratch bucket encrypts by default, and no check reads a restored object's SSE state**, so dropping `--enc-s3` from the restore's `mc pipe` passes every check. Production buckets keep the default, so it is a gap in the control, not a live exposure. **Its scratch bucket loses the default, `mc stat` checks each restored object, and the mutant is seen red in CI's backup step.** `test-engineer`, at the end of lane 7. If it changes `.github/workflows/`, record 24 has opened that gate *(Merged 2026-11-09 as [#929](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/929), 76 added lines, 0.10× its top, closing #921. `ci.yml` is not changed: the probe's `backup` step already runs in CI; §5's twenty-ninth state note.)* |
| 48 *(added 2026-11-10)* | **A `calculate` that reads the whole record is refused at a form's publish** | [#936](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/936) | **D-137** B, answered 2026-11-10 (§8), raised by row 42. In a `calculate`, `{"var":""}` is `CALCULATION_CYCLE` at save, while `{"var":null}`, `{"var":[]}` and `{"var":[null]}` read the same whole record and have no edge. **A form's publish refuses the three, `EXPRESSION_PATH_COMPUTED`, in `calculate` only**; a `conditional` reading the whole record is no cycle and stays accepted, and a form already published is not re-checked. **`{"var":[null, X]}` is refused too, by the product owner's answer on #936, if AC-48-4's probe shows it reads the whole record**; #822 had pinned it as accepted, and the answer is the row's own question, so it takes no `D-n`. **Below the line, outside §3.2's count**, as rows 37, 38, 40, 41, 45, 46 and 47 are; #936 has no priority, the product owner's. ~~About 150 lines, D-137's figure.~~ **About 190–230 lines, by [its criteria](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/936#issuecomment-6762587849)**, AC-48-1 to AC-48-14, against D-137's about 150 (struck and restated 2026-11-10). `rust-backend`, in lane 5; ~~**once `requirements-analyst`'s criteria are posted on #936** (§9)~~ **the criteria are posted, and it is being built** on `fix/calculate-whole-record` (struck and restated 2026-11-10); **`schema-guardian`** if a JFSS or registry text changes, as for row 42. It changes `rad/`, one of §1.2's six modules, and record 24 has opened that gate. If it has not merged at Sprint 25's close it goes where row 37 would have gone (§5's first state note) *(Merged 2026-11-10 as [#938](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/938), 521 added lines, 2.27× its top, closing #936. AC-48-4's probe found that `{"var":[null, X]}` answers `null` and reads nothing, so it stays accepted. #938 merged before its `test-engineer` campaign ran, which is in progress on `test/row-48-campaign`, and before its other three gates, which ran after it and owe JFSS erratum E-7 and SDD and User Manual texts, in a follow-up docs change from `main`; §5's thirty-second state note.)* |
| 49 *(added 2026-11-10)* | **A display's `calculate` that reads the whole record as an empty path is refused at a form's publish** | [#941](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/941) | **D-147** A, answered 2026-11-10 (§8), raised from row 48's campaign. On a display, `{"var": null}`, `{"var": []}` and `{"var": [null]}` in a `calculate` are refused since row 48, and `{"var": ""}` and `{"var": [""]}` publish, since a display is not a node of JFSS S12.2's graph. **A form's publish refuses the `""` family on a non-data component too, `EXPRESSION_PATH_COMPUTED`, outside an iterator's body**; a form already published is not re-checked, and #942's `#[ignore]`d test is un-ignored. **JFSS takes erratum E-8 in its change**, so **`schema-guardian`** reads it at the gate. **Below the line, outside §3.2's count**, as rows 37, 38, 40, 41, 45, 46, 47 and 48 are; #941 has no priority, the product owner's, and carries `sprint:25`. **200–450 lines, by [its criteria](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/941#issuecomment-6768850726)**, AC-49-1 to AC-49-10, with a stop at 750. `rust-backend`, in lane 5; **`requirements-analyst`'s criteria are posted on #941**, so it can be assigned. It changes `rad/`, one of §1.2's six modules, and record 24 has opened that gate. ~~**Placed in Sprint 25 and not built.** If it has not merged at Sprint 25's close it goes where row 37 would have gone (§5's first state note), to Sprint 26 before its freeze, and plan 22 orders it there~~ **Placed in Sprint 25, and merged there** (struck and restated 2026-11-12) *(§5's thirty-fourth state note)* *(Merged 2026-11-12 as [#946](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/946), 634 added lines, 1.41× its top and under its stop at 750, with JFSS erratum E-8. **Every gate ran before the merge**, and the campaign's seven mutants are all killed. **#941 ~~is still open~~ was closed later that day, at the product owner's word.** [#947](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/947) and [#948](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/948) were filed as follow-ups, unprioritised and unplaced; §5's thirty-fifth state note.)* |
| 50 *(added 2026-11-13)* | **A display's `calculate` and every non-data role's `conditional.logic` are checked against the registry** | [#948](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/948) | **D-149** A, raised and answered 2026-11-12 (§8), after the product owner chose #948, row 49's follow-up, as row 50. `check_operator_sets` walked data components only, so a save of `{"Var": ""}` on a display returned 201. **The gap was a regression**: #344 (`4312a81`, 2026-09-05) moved the operator walk into the data-only `ScopeCatalogue`, and the walk it replaced read every role. **A display's `calculate` meets the [Calculation Rule Registry](../../docs/schema/JFSS%20Calculation%20Rule%20Registry.md), and every non-data role's `conditional.logic` meets the `conditional` floor, the registry's §2.5, at save and at publish.** **`should`, with `sprint:25`, below the line, outside §3.2's count**, as rows 37, 38, 40, 41 and 45 to 49 are. **250–500 lines, by [its criteria](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/948#issuecomment-6825241034)**, AC-50-1 to AC-50-12, with a stop at 750. `rust-backend`, in lane 5; `doc-author` for the registry, SDD and User Manual texts; **`schema-guardian`** reads the registry at the gate. It changes `rad/`, one of §1.2's six modules, and record 24 has opened that gate *(§5's thirty-sixth state note)* *(Merged 2026-11-13 as [#950](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/950), 1,001 added lines, 2.00× its top and **1.33× its stop at 750**, kept whole by the product owner's choice when the main session stopped and asked. The registry goes to 1.7.2, a patch. **Every gate ran before the merge**, and the campaign's ten mutants are all killed. **#948 was closed by the main session after the merge**, since #950 said *Refs*. Nothing was filed; §5's thirty-sixth state note.)* |

**Lanes 3–7 are parallel-safe with one another as work. Merges are not.** Rows that add to `CHANGELOG.md`'s `[Unreleased]`, or change the same `docs/` file, merge one after the other: **whichever merges second is rebased onto the first, and its checks run again on that tree.**

  - **What is on `main` now**: a save refuses a group repeated without an upper bound whose body is one item itself repeated without one, as in `(a+)+`, or an alternation, as in `(cat|dog)+`. The answer is `PATTERN_BACKTRACKING_SHAPE`, wherever `check_patterns` runs. **That narrow rule is the product owner's choice of 2026-10-30 within D-93 A**, at the main session's question, and not a new decision. A body of more than one item, as in `([a-z]+\.)+`, is stored. **The check names two shapes and claims nothing about what it accepts**: `(a+b?)+` is stored and is exponential, and the texts say so.
  - **A stored definition reads back and its documents work**, and its next revision is refused. `CHANGELOG.md`'s *Upgrade notes* say so. ADR-0038 is amended, the Validation Rule Registry is 1.7.0, and the form no longer tells a reader the server decides a `client` rule the browser gave up on. **The renderer half lane 6 held for this row rode in the same change.**

Each is a 422 where it was a 500. **The login and forgot-password routes were reachable with no token.** It changes the code on two shapes: a NUL in a uuid capture moves from 400 to 422 on about 96 routes, and `/rad/lookups/%00/options` from 404 to 422.

    - **The scan went to every role**: `calculate` and `conditional.logic` on every component of every role, layouts' and displays' included, with a computed `missing` or `missing_some` name and the scope jump refused beside a computed `var`.

    - **[The criteria](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/822#issuecomment-6537486165)'s AC11, *not an erratum*, was overturned by `schema-guardian`.** They were posted on 2026-10-31 at 01:57:52 UTC and corrected at the build for the positions, and that comment does not say AC11 was overturned.

A computed `var` still gets no edge, a draft's save does not check it, and a form already published is not re-checked. `CHANGELOG.md`'s *Upgrade notes* say what to look for. **Two things it leaves are named below**: **D-137** and [#828](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/828).

- **Merged: row 44, [#834](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/834), by [#848](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/848)** (`c914bec`, *perf(attachment): stream the upload, the download and the scan*), at 18:23:42 UTC on 2026-11-01. **It closed #834.**
  - **Measured: 3,967 added lines** (and 222 deleted), in 25 files. **Outside its 700–1,400, at 2.83× the top, 2,567 lines past it, and the furthest over of any row this sprint.** By §3.1 rule 7 the range stays, and the sum and the line absorb the overrun.
  - **Where it went**: 2,804 in tests, 2,800 of them `attachment_streams.rs`; 1,027 in `src/`, unit tests included; 124 in texts; 12 in `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock`. **The estimate was short on both halves.** It had no comparable, so it was sized by its columns, and they held neither the three timeouts streaming made necessary nor what the abort paths cost in tests: every `await` on the upload's road is dropped alone against real MinIO.
  - **The decision.** **D-140 was raised on 2026-11-01 from row 44's criteria and answered the same day by the product owner**, the recommendation as written ([Product Backlog](02.%20Product%20Backlog.md) §6.1): stream the download, the scan worker and the upload. Accepting the buffering would have taken about 910–990 off the line as then projected, and that was the part that could move. It no longer can.
  - **The verdict.** **NFR-PERF-004 is met with stated limits.** For 25 MiB the most held went from 26,214,552 bytes to 5,624,946 for the upload, from 26,214,400 to 109,664 for the download, and from 26,214,400 to 3,505,084 for the worker. **Only the file stopped following the cap.** A download that fails after its headers truncates, and `Attachment.Downloaded` can name it. A reader that stops for a long time can lose the transfer. A client that stalls mid-upload holds its socket, one part and one open upload, because no request timeout exists in the backend or in `deploy/`. A process killed mid-upload leaves hidden parts, never an object.
  - **What moved for a caller.** `test-engineer` ran 45 fault sets on `4683c3d` and on the branch. All 9 single faults answer as they did. A caller with no permission or no document gets 403 or 404 ahead of a body fault, and past one part a store that fails answers 500 ahead of a later fault. **AC8 was wrong on one**: a disallowed type before a malformed `categoryId` did not move.
  - **What its PR says of itself**: not independent verification. `test-engineer` ran 35 mutations, 32 red, and found one defect, fixed in the branch: a request dropped while the store opened the upload left an incomplete upload that MinIO listed. Three scanner writes survive with no test. `requirements-analyst` read and did not run, and its reading that the type refusal moved was wrong against the code. `doc-consistency` did not re-read the last two text commits.
  - **CI settled what was left to it.** All six checks passed at `0000c12`, the full backend suite in 49m01s among them, and the 11 extra databases of the dropped-request test did not cost a run there.

**State note, 2026-11-02, the thirteenth.** *Two changes merged after the twelfth note: [#850](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/850), which carried it, and row 1, [#851](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/851). This note records both, restates the line with row 1 at its measured figure, and is carried by its own change.*

- **How each figure is read.** As the first twelve notes: `git show --numstat` of each squash commit, added lines, less `.sqlx/` and `package-lock.json`. #851 is the first of these changes with a `package-lock.json` of its own, 81 lines at `load/`, left out by that rule. **Every git and tracker figure here was read by the main session on 2026-11-02.**
- **Merged, not a row: [#850](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/850)** (`8478faa`), at 00:08:52 UTC on 2026-11-02. It carried the twelfth note, so this one gives its figure (§5's rule). **Measured: 52 added lines** (and 20 deleted), in 4 files.
- **Merged: row 1, [#777](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/777), by [#851](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/851)** (`e56a0b4`, *test(load): nfr-perf-001 measured; both views meet their targets*), at 08:19:17 UTC on 2026-11-02. **It closed #777.**
  - **Measured: 3,002 added lines** (3,083 with the lock file, and 4 deleted), in 21 files. **Outside its 1,230–2,450, at 1.23× the top, 552 lines past it.** By §3.1 rule 7 the range stays, and the sum and the line absorb the overrun.
  - **Where it went, by §3.2's columns**: scenarios 369 against 300–600, seeder 487 against 300–700, view spec 361 against 300–500, and texts 212 against 200–350, each inside. **The runner is 1,573 against 130–300, 5.24× its top, and more than the whole of the overrun**, because the other columns sit under their tops: the column sized a script that starts a stack, and the runner also proves its own captures, voids a run that breaks a stated rule, reports, and carries 224 lines of tests. Nothing under `kelir-backend`, `kelir-frontend`, `deploy`, `.github`, `e2e` or `scripts` changed, so the row measures and does not fix, as §4 says.
  - **The verdict. NFR-PERF-001 is met on the laptop, on one seeded data set, under named limits.** A list's target is 1,000 ms: the scenario's P95 is 41 ms (n=7,326) and Chromium's 70.9 ms (n=30). A detail's is 2,000 ms: the scenario's P95 is 19 ms (n=7,327) and Chromium's 51.0 ms (n=30). The run of record is `target-1793599343160`, on product `8478faa` and harness `37b8954`, and [its record on #777](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/777#issuecomment-6570688144) and SDD §13.4 give the rest. **At zero think time, a reading not compared with the target, the limit is the laptop's CPU, not the pool.** **The figures `v1.0.0` states are Sprint 26's**, on the release candidate (§3.3).
  - **What the reading does not show.** Opening a tab after the page is a second wait, and Chromium never timed it. PostgreSQL ran in a container on default settings without TLS, and the edge was plain HTTP/1.1, over loopback for Chromium. No document has an entity, attachments are rows without objects, the activity tab is empty, and all 500 typical documents are `IN_REVIEW`. The host was not idle, and 30 samples are thin: Chromium's detail P95 went 68.8 → 51.0 ms between otherwise like runs, undiagnosed.
  - **AC2 was wrong as written.** It says the detail page's tabs fetch at once. `DocumentWorkspace.vue` mounts each with a `v-if`, and it did when AC2 was written. The scenario follows the page, and the correction is on #777.
  - **The triggers.** ADR-0045 §6 did not fire: all ten connections were active in 0 of 1,500 samples at target and 33 of 1,500 at saturation. ADR-0048 §6 was read for sign-in only and did not fire, because no scenario refreshes a token or writes a `users` row. **No breach, so nothing is filed, and #771 does not move** (§8's condition 4).

  - **Measured: 446 added lines** (and 2 deleted), in 6 files. **Inside its 350–750, at 0.59× the top and 1.27× the bottom.**

  - **What it fixes.** A loaded bare `!`, such as `{"!": {"var": "a"}}`, given an array typed into its operand's *Advanced (JSON)* box, was saved as `{"!": [0, 1]}`, which JSON Logic reads as `!0`. **`emit` now wraps an array operand, `{"!": [[0, 1]]}`, whatever its length**, and the node stays bare, so a scalar typed after it returns to the bare form. **D-110's promise holds on this edit**: nothing loaded is rewritten. Measured on `datalogic-wasm`: `[0, 1]` and `[0]` give `false` wrapped and `true` bare, and `[]` gives `true` both ways. A rule already saved in the old form is not repaired.

- **How each figure is read.** As the first fifteen notes: `git show --numstat` of each squash commit, added lines, less `.sqlx/` and `package-lock.json`, of which neither change has any. **Every git and tracker figure here was read by the main session on 2026-11-03.**
- **Merged, not a row: [#862](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/862)** (`35681b1`), at 05:32:37 UTC on 2026-11-03. It carried the fifteenth note, so this one gives its figure (§5's rule). **Measured: 50 added lines** (and 19 deleted), in 4 files.
- **Merged: row 36, [#790](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/790), by [#863](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/863)** (`5dc726c`, *fix(form-builder): an options edit keeps the values it left alone*), at 08:13:44 UTC on 2026-11-03. **It closed #790.**
  - **Measured: 890 added lines** (and 51 deleted), in 9 files. **Outside its 350–750, at 1.19× the top, 140 lines past it.** By §3.1 rule 7 the range stays, and the sum and the line absorb the overrun.
  - **Where it went**: 188 feature lines, 140 of them the new `OptionRows.vue`, 37 `propertyEdits.ts` and 11 `DataFieldProperties.vue`, **against the feature column's 20–80, 2.35× its top**; 685 in tests, across four specs; and 17 in texts, the CHANGELOG and the User Manual. **The campaign's commit was 295 of them, all tests**, with no production change. Nothing under `kelir-backend`, `deploy`, `.github`, `e2e` or `scripts` changed.
  - **§3.1 rule 1 did not hold this time.** Plan 20's row 11 was 494 with 13 feature lines; this row is 890 with 188. **§3.2's column sized a fix to the box's lines, and the criteria chose a row per option instead**, because the main session's probe showed the box's binding also broke typing: a newline typed at the end vanished, and a bare label became `label=label` at the first keystroke. A remedy that kept the binding kept that.
  - **What it fixes.** An edit to a select's or radio's *Options* box rewrote every option, so every value was saved as text, `1` as `"1"` and `true` as `"true"`, and a label holding `=` was split. **Each option is now a row, with a label, a value and *Remove*, and an edit replaces only its own row's option**, so every other option is saved as loaded. A typed value is read by the field's `validation.type`; one that does not read as a `number`, `integer` or `boolean` is saved as the text typed, with a note on the row, and is never refused. A value that is not text is shown with its type. **D-110's promise holds on this edit**, and #732's AC4 is pinned for options. No API, backend or JFSS change, so `schema-guardian` was not owed.
  - **The campaign.** The builder's 13 mutants all failed its suite. `test-engineer` ran 26 more: 21 killed, 9 by the builder's suite and 12 by cases added in a commit of their own; 4 argued equivalent; and 1 a finding. **No production defect inside the row.** Two of the gaps it closed were real failures nothing had caught: **rows keyed by label lost focus at every keystroke**, and **without a key on the page's panel, one select's half-typed value showed on the next select**.
  - **Recorded and not filed**, the criteria being silent on each: a value is read as `Number` reads it, so `1e3` is saved as 1000, `0x10` as 16 and `' 1 '` as 1, while `' true '` stays text; and an integer typed past 2^53 loses precision silently. **`switchType` keeps `options` when a select becomes a text field**, which its doc comment states, so it is not a defect.
  - **What it does not show.** No browser ran the rows: everything is jsdom, and no `e2e/` spec types into options. The mutants ran on the form-builder and page specs, and the full suite ran unmutated.
  - **What its PR says of itself**: not independent verification. `doc-consistency` found one blocker, the User Manual's stale *Last updated*, fixed with its two wording points. `requirements-analyst` read AC3–AC5 in full and the rest by title and code, ran nothing, and found nothing blocking; it corrected the build report, since a fresh row on a `number` field shows no note until a value is typed. **Neither re-read the last wording fixes.**
  - **CI.** All six checks passed at `ab2365b`, with the backend suite in 39m06s and the browser job in 11m26s.

  - **Measured: 13 added lines** (and 7 deleted), all texts: architectures/01 4, the EES 6 and LHCS 3. **Under its 40–200, at 0.33× the bottom, 27 lines under it.** §3.2 sizes it by rule 2, unpinned, with no comparable figure, as it sized row 18, so none is named.
  - **What is on `main` now.** LHCS §4, revision R-4, and the EES §4.1, R-3, each say that a payload's `documentTypeKey` is `document_types.type_code` exactly as stored: conventionally upper-case, as DTDS §2 normalizes it, and never DTDS's lower-case key. **The two payload examples, architectures/01 §12.2's and the EES §7's, read `PURCHASE_REQUISITION`**, each with a dated note. The other two blocks #768 names, architectures/01 §10.4 and §12.4.1, are DTDS definitions, whose `key` DTDS §2's pattern requires in lower case, so they stay as they are. No shape, version or meta-schema `pattern` changed. No outbox envelope carries the key today, and R-3 says so.
  - **What the texts could not say.** `POST /document-types` does not upper-case a `typeCode`, so both sentences call the case conventional, not enforced, and cite [#873](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/873), filed for the product owner's choice.
  - **Gate**: **`schema-guardian`** found no blockers and judged the author's five choices acceptable; its point that the sentences read as if the case were enforced was fixed in the second commit, which it did not re-read. `doc-consistency` found no blockers; its one note, a lower-case key in architectures/07's governance block, is not a payload and was left. §7's gate row for 28 has no *Before* step. **Not independent verification.** All six CI checks passed on the head that merged, and `main`'s run at `bb42cec`, which holds it, passed (merge order, below).

  - **What it fixes.** The renderer read a missing `pattern` or `target` as the text `"undefined"`: a `regex` rule with no `pattern` refused nearly every value, and a field rule with no `target` read a field keyed `undefined`. **It now reads a missing parameter as `""`, as the server does.** A `null` stays `"null"`, as the server's `js_string` reads it, and `flags` is unchanged; a non-string `flags` still splits the two sides until the server half refuses one, and the code comment says so (AC18).

  - **What is on `main` now.** The pin test reads a `FROM` continued over an empty line, as BuildKit joins it, and refuses a `FROM` that names no image. It holds `COPY --from` and `RUN --mount=…,from=` to the `FROM` rule, reads `# syntax=` in the leading directive block, finds `Dockerfile-<word>`, `Containerfile-<word>`, `*.containerfile` and `Containerfile.*`, reads compose `dockerfile_inline` in block, one-line, flow and quoted-key form, and reads `deploy/**/*.sh` for floating tags. The routes it does not read are named in its header with the reason, and the Installation guide names the same. It has 12 tests, up from 8. `check:bundle` gives Vue Flow a `stylesheetMarker`, `.vue-flow__`, and searches every first-load stylesheet for it: the chunks' CSS, `<link rel=stylesheet>`, `rel=preload as=style`, `<style>` in `dist/index.html` and followed `@import`s. It also fails a remote import or link naming a subject package, and a marker no stylesheet credited to the subject carries. ADR-0046 §5 says so.

  - **What is on `main` now.** The claimed arm of `with_claimed_clause!`, one text spliced into the inbox list, its count and the detail gate, counts a `USER` edge as resolving only when its `userId` is a JSON string, in one of the four forms the engine's `uuid` parser accepts, naming a user of the task's tenant with `deleted_at IS NULL`; `users.status` is not read, as the engine does not read it. An `OWNER` edge resolves only when `documents.created_by` is such a user, the opposite of `OWNER_DEPARTMENT`'s reading. A value is cast only after it matches, so no stored `userId` can raise, and a spelling only PostgreSQL accepts does not resolve. A claimed task with no resolving edge leaves its holder's inbox, counts and dashboard, and its page answers 404; the unclaimed arm is unchanged. **A third kind of hidden task**, whose edges name no role, is found by nothing yet: the remedy is the data, by SQL, and a `userId` that is not a uuid, or names another tenant's user or nobody, has none. The `inbox.rs` header, SDD §8.5.3, User Manual §7.1, the Installation guide §9 and ADR-0042 §4 say so.
  - **Gate**: §7's for row 23. Its criteria were posted on #762 before the build. **`test-engineer`: criteria 1–9 and 12 PASS.** The oracle has 44 cases and was seen red on 23 against `main`'s clause. Of 28 mutants of the clause, 26 were killed. The 2 survivors were the hex class loosened, under which a 36-character non-hex `userId` made the inbox answer 500, and the `USER` type test dropped; the campaign's own commit, `82d7197`, kills both. **Its probes found one gap**: a `userId` stored as a JSON number whose 32 decimal digits spell a live user's id resolved in the clause, while the engine reads a number as absent. That broke criterion 2, so it was fixed in the row, not filed: `323c1a1` requires a JSON string, with an oracle row seen red first. The campaign's re-check passed: of five mutants of that guard, three were killed and two argued equivalent. `doc-consistency`: no blockers, four points, all fixed in `ae6d9b4` and not re-read. `task_inbox` passed 115/115 and `workflow_engine` 77/77, and the full backend suite 2,721 at `cf0a38e`, its 12 git-reading tests passing in the git image. **`requirements-analyst` did not run before the merge**; it ran after it, on 2026-11-05, read and not run. **FR-TASK-001 and 002 trace from the SRS through SDD §8.5.3 to the clause and the oracle: PASS.** FR-WF-017 is traced only as the texts' claim that a reassign of such a task is `TARGET_CANNOT_DECIDE`, and **two claims the texts make have no test**: that refusal for a person edge, and that the data remedy brings a task back. **Criterion 11 failed on wording**: ADR-0042's traceability row did not name #893, and the CHANGELOG bullet read as if only the kind with no remedy lacked a finder.   - **What is on `main` now.** Both checks run inside `validate_definition`, so save, update, a revision with a body, and publish all refuse them. **A `regex` rule's `flags` that is present and not a string** (`null`, a boolean, a number, an array or an object) is `RULE_PARAM_INVALID` at `…rules.M.params.flags`, and its pattern is not judged too; an absent `flags` and `""` still store, and a missing pattern beside a bad `flags` is still two defects, each at its own path. **An empty `validation.pattern`** is `RULE_PARAM_MISSING` at `…validation.pattern`, reported once and not judged as a pattern too; no new code is added. **Published revisions and submit are unchanged**: a stored revision of either shape still takes documents, and is refused at its next revision or at the publish of a copy. The Validation Rule Registry is at **1.8.0**, and JFSS takes **erratum E-6**, its meta-schema unchanged.

  - **What is on `main` now.** The inbox clause no longer reads the caller, so a claimed task and an unclaimed one are judged alike. **An open, unclaimed task none of whose decision edges resolves leaves the list, the count and every visibility check**, and **a claim of it is refused**, 422 `ASSIGNMENT_UNRESOLVED` at `allowedBy`, checked under the instance lock after the role check, so a caller without the role still gets 403, and nothing is written. A task with one resolving edge, or with no decision edge, as a `RETURNED` correction, stays visible and claimable. A claimed task that has become undecidable answers a claim with 422, not 409. JWSS takes R-22, with no shape change, and ADR-0042 §4, SDD §8.5.3, the Installation and Deployment guide §9, the User Manual §7.1 and `CHANGELOG.md` say so. Ten existing tests that claimed a task whose edges never resolve now go through a helper, `claim_or_place`.
  - **Gate**: §7's for row 29, its criteria posted on #761 before the build.
    - **The builder**: the flipped tests and the oracle's new rows seen red first against #905's code, and mutants M1–M6 killed. `cargo fmt` and clippy were clean, and the touched suites passed locally, on 4 jobs.
    - **`test-engineer`: no production defect, and three blind spots closed in `c176ea0`**, tests only. `claim_or_place` hid an inverted claim from 22 of the 24 tests that reach it; it now checks that the task is hidden before it falls back, and 20 of the 22 fail under that mutant. P3, the undecidable check before the role check, and P5, the edges read before the lock, survived, and a new test kills each, both red first against `594c07b`. P4 is argued equivalent.
    - **`doc-consistency`: no blocker.** Its should-fix, an upgrade note in `CHANGELOG.md`, and its five nits are applied in `713a9b3`. That commit also corrects a claim in `inbox.rs` and `service/task.rs`: the instance lock orders a claim against the task's own transitions, not against a delete of the data an edge names.
    - **`requirements-analyst`: mergeable after one should-fix**, the claim route's OpenAPI 422, applied in `16def6d`. AC1–AC5 were verified against the code, and AC6–AC8 rest on the builder's and the campaign's reports.

  - **The gate**, by its PR's account. **`test-engineer`, in `b3fd916`, tests only: no production defect.** On every case built through the API or by SQL, the raise refuses exactly the tasks the inbox clause hides. Four plausible mutants survived the builder's suites: M1 and M2, a user's or a department's `status` read as well as its `deleted_at`; M3, `OWNER_DEPARTMENT` reading the creator's `deleted_at`; and M6, conditioned edges skipped. `the_raise_and_the_inbox_agree_beyond_deletion`, 29 rows, kills all four. S1, a function-value use of `insert_task`, passed the builder's source test, and a new test kills it; M10 is argued equivalent. `submit_or_place` now checks that the clause agrees the original edges are dead, as `claim_or_place` does, and it masked no fixture. Runs: lib 1,062, `task_inbox` 122 and `workflow_engine` 88, all passing.
    - **`doc-consistency`'s should-fix and nits, in `b629d97`**: JWSS line 181 now reads *submit, resubmit, decision or reassign*; `CHANGELOG.md`'s stray blank line and wrap, the SDD's links to ADR-0042 and the Installation guide, and `inbox.rs`'s header.

The stale D-141 register row it flagged was restated by #913.

    1. the named machine is the one this work runs on, a 12th Gen Intel Core i5-12450H, not #800's Ryzen 7 4800H, so #800's figures stay as history;
    2. the load target is read on a page clock, because about 30% of Playwright's `load_ms` at 300 states is its own trace snapshotter;
    3. the DOM targets count with CDP's `Memory.getDOMCounters`;
    4. the tab switch is read on the page clock.

    **The threshold is more than 40 states or more than 112 transitions**, the largest size whose before load was at most 1 s, and above every real draft, the largest of which is 31 states. Neither variant that needs the product owner, collapsed always or an enforced limit, was built, so no `D-n` was raised. **Erratum**: AC-38-8 names the User Manual's §10.5, which is *Numbering*; the text went into §10.6, *Workflows*. **Added at the gates**: a state the author adds to a folded list opens at once, under AC-38-3.

That is more than 892 × 300, and the excess is unexplained.

  - **What it does.** **D-137** B, as answered. Once a definition is otherwise valid, a form's publish refuses a `calculate` whose `var` operand is `null`, `[]` or `[null]`, outside an iterator's body, as 422 `VALIDATION_ERROR` with one `EXPRESSION_PATH_COMPUTED` detail at its path, `rule: "expressionPath"`, and a message of its own that names no path. **In `calculate` only**: a `conditional` reading the whole record is still accepted. Saving a draft does not check it, and a form already published is not re-checked. No migration, and JFSS's meta-schema is untouched.
  - **The probe, AC-48-4.** It was run with the backend's `RuleEvaluator`, on datalogic-rs 5.2.0, against a two-field record. **`null`, `[]` and `[null]` each answer the whole record. `[null, "a"]` answers `null`, takes no default and reads nothing.** So `{"var":[null, X]}` does not read the whole record, and **by the product owner's conditional answer on #936 it stays accepted**; #822's pin of it stands, and no `D-n` is owed. The probe is kept as a unit test, seen red first against a wrong expectation, the record's `a` for all four, which printed the four answers it asserts.
  - **Measured: 521 added lines** (and 26 deleted), across 6 files: the rad engine, the form service, `tests/rad_forms.rs`, `CHANGELOG.md`, JFSS and SDD. **409 are tests** (229 in the engine's unit module, 180 in `tests/rad_forms.rs`), **92 production** (88 in the engine, 4 in the form service) and **20 texts** (`CHANGELOG.md` 18, JFSS 1, SDD 1).

- **D-149, raised and answered 2026-11-12: A, the recommendation as written** ([Product Backlog](02.%20Product%20Backlog.md) §6.1; §8).
  - **The choice.** The product owner chose **#948 as row 50**: *rad: a display's calculate skips the operator registry*, filed on 2026-11-12 as row 49's follow-up and named in the thirty-fifth note.
  - **The question**, drafted by `requirements-analyst`: are a display's and a layout's `calculate` and `conditional.logic` held to the [Calculation Rule Registry](../../docs/schema/JFSS%20Calculation%20Rule%20Registry.md) and the `conditional` floor, as a data field's are? **A**: a display's `calculate` meets the registry, and every non-data role's `conditional.logic` meets the floor, the registry's §2.5, at save and at publish. **B**: `conditional` only. **C**: leave it, and document it. **D**: A, plus a display tier that admits `cat`.
  - **What A decides.** A display's `calculate` meets the registry, and every non-data role's `conditional.logic` meets the `conditional` floor, **at save and at publish**, as a data field's do.
  - **The gap was a regression.** [#344](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/344) (`4312a81`, 2026-09-05) moved the operator walk into the data-only `ScopeCatalogue`. The walk it replaced read every role.
  - **It never stood in §6.2**: it was raised and answered the same day, as D-148 was.

### 7.1 Record 24's brief

*Written by the project manager as a subagent of the main session, so it carries that session's ID. The reader's §1 reads that ID from the `Claude-Session:` line of the commit that lands this plan, and names it as the point of contact. The product owner pastes this into a new top-level session and starts it.*

> **You are the independent reader of Sprint 24.** You write verification record 24. You fix nothing, and you author no commit outside `projects/verifications/`.
>
> **Who may be this reader.** A top-level Claude Code session the product owner starts, never a subagent ([sprint plan](01.%20Sprint%20Plan.md) §2 (b)). Your ID is in no `Claude-Session:` line of a construction commit since `v0.9.0`, **and it stays out of every construction commit until `v1.0.0`**, because `v1.0.0`'s record may cite you, and the gate's rule 14 refuses a cited reader who wrote construction in the release's range. You may dispatch read-only subagents. They commit nothing, edit no tracked file and file nothing, and you re-read each of their candidates against the code yourself.
>
> **§1 first, before you read anything.**
> - Print `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`. Your header's second line is `**Reader session:** <that full id>` (rule 13).
> - List every `Claude-Session:` line from `v0.9.0` to `HEAD`, with its commit. **Count them with `grep` over the commit bodies**: git's trailer parser reads none of them on a squash commit. **Your ID must be in none**, unless the only commits it is on change nothing outside `projects/verifications/`. If it is on a construction commit, stop and tell the product owner.
> - Check the range for the shapes the session-line check misses ([#557](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/557)): a Claude co-author with no session line; one hidden after an unclosed fence; a one-parent commit titled `Merge …`; a session line inside a fenced block. Name each one, or say there are none.
> - Name this brief's author and point of contact: the main session whose ID is on the commit that lands construction plan 21.
> - **Record the commit `main` is at when you start**, and the time. Status report 26 sets them beside Sprint 25's first merge.
>
> **The range is `59b42fe..a9f1cdf`: [#724](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/724) to [#772](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/772), 21 squash commits, 29,111 lines** ([status report 25](../status/25.%20Sprint%2024%20Status.md)). **Seventeen change code**: #727, #729, #732, #734, #738, #740, #741, #743, #744, #745, #746, #754, #757, #760, #766, #770 and #772. **Four are documents**: #724 and #726, which landed and adopted plan 20; #752, which is record 23 (name it, and do not re-read it); and #753, texts with the comments of one test file and one source file. [#775](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/775), the close, is outside the range. **Your file is `projects/verifications/24. Sprint 24 Independent Pass.md`.** Its name says `Sprint 24`. **Your PR changes that file and nothing else**, and the project manager adds the index row after it merges.
>
> **Where you work.** Not in the main checkout: make a worktree of your own from `origin/main`. **A `kelir-staging` stack runs on port 8090. Do not use it or replace it**; if you bring a stack up, use a compose project of your own on spare ports. The backend tests run against the native PostgreSQL 17 on port 5432, role and database `kelir_dev`, never against staging. The integration tests need eight environment variables, `SQLX_OFFLINE` and the object store's and the scanner's ports, and a flood of E0282 means the environment, not the code. If the antivirus blocks `sqlx_macros` or a test binary (os error 225, or a binary with no result line), ask the product owner to restore it; rebuilding does not help. If the full suite is stopped for memory, run the missing binaries by name: **a binary with no result line did not finish.** A worktree has no `node_modules`. **Probes are tests added to existing files and never committed**; `git status` is clean before you write the record. State what you could not execute before you state what you found.
>
> **Your subjects, in this order.**
>
> 1. **[#770](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/770), the condition paths, and what it changed beside its row.** **Told**: **D-115** B built `document.documentTypeKey`, `actor.roles` and `actor.departmentId`; a publish refuses a path the engine does not build (JWSS R-17); and, by the main session's call, `identity::repository::role_codes_for_user` now honours a grant's window, so **a sign-in token's `roles` claim no longer lists an expired or not-yet-valid grant**. **Asked**: does a publish refuse every unbuilt path, and only those? **A gate that fails sends the row back to its builder with the findings.** Every gate above is a subagent of the session that dispatches it, so **none of them is an independent reader**. Nothing reads Sprint 25 inside Sprint 25, so plan 22 places record 25.

### Merge order

**A dependent PR is opened only after its predecessor has merged, or as a GitHub draft that stays a draft until then.** Status report 26 lists each pair with both merge times.

| Dependent | Waits for | Why |
|---|---|---|
| **Every row that changes one of §1.2's six modules** | **Record 24's PR, and its blocking fixes** | §1.2; retrospective 22, second action |
| Row 2's build | **Row 2's ADR**, in the same PR or before it | The ADR decides the shape |
| Row 4 | **Row 1** | It runs on row 1's harness *(Row 1 merged as #851, 2026-11-02)* |
| Row 6 | **Its ADR**, in the same PR | It adds a lock |
| Rows 19–31 | **Their lane free**, row 22 first, and for rows 29 and 30 their questions answered | Below the line. *(2026-10-31: the refinement comes before them, §7 steps 5 and 6, and rows held for it keep their lane from being free; §5's eighth state note.)* *(2026-10-31, later: the refinement is taken and no row is held for it, so row 22 goes first once lane 6's rows 10 and 36 have merged; §5's ninth state note.)* *(Row 10 merged as #860, 2026-11-03; row 36 remains; §5's fifteenth state note.)* *(Row 36 merged as #863, 2026-11-03, so both have merged and row 22 can start; §5's sixteenth state note.)* *(Row 22 merged as #868, 2026-11-04, the first of them, so rows 19–31 now wait only for their lane, and 29 and 30 for their questions too; §5's seventeenth state note.)* *(Rows 28, 25 and 19 and row 24's renderer half merged as #874, #878, #879 and #876, 2026-11-04; §5's eighteenth state note.)* *(Row 20 merged as #882, 2026-11-04; §5's nineteenth state note.)* *(Row 21 merged as #891, 2026-11-05, without a rebase; §5's twentieth state note.)* *(Row 23 merged as #894, 2026-11-05, without a rebase; §5's twenty-first state note.)* *(Row 24's server half merged as #896, 2026-11-05, without a rebase; §5's twenty-second state note.)* *(2026-11-06: row 29's four questions were answered, so only row 30 waits for its question; #763 merged as #905; §5's twenty-fifth state note.)* *(2026-11-07: #908, #910 and #912 merged, so row 29 is whole, and row 30 is the only one of them left, waiting for its question; §5's twenty-sixth state note.)* |
| Row 3's record of NFR-SEC-010 *(added 2026-10-28)* | **Row 39** | Under **D-119** C the NFR is met only when the database hop requires TLS. *(Satisfied 2026-10-29: row 39 merged as [#808](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/808). Row 3 has not started; §5's fourth state note.)* *(Row 3 merged as #840, 2026-10-31; §5's tenth state note.)* |

**The *not ready* line, until `Pull-request ready` is required.** A feature row's PR is a draft whose first line says it is not ready, and **the main session reports it ready only when its body carries the campaign section, and every follow-up its body names carries an issue number** (row 18's rule, ~~applied before it merges~~ in the git workflow §4 since [#866](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/866), 2026-11-04; struck and restated that day). That is plan 18 §7.3's sentence. Status report 26 lists every merge whose body still carried the line, with both times.

## 8. Decisions this plan takes, and the one it owes

**Decided by this plan** (sequence and shape, not scope):

1. **The line is set against 20,714, and the rows below it run to 29,111** (§3), because rows 1 and 2 have no comparable.
2. **§3.1's rules change**: a pinned text row is 550–900, and an unpinned one 40–200; the workflow-seam range is 1,200–2,500; a race row is 1,250–1,900; and no row is padded for its campaign.
3. **#721's ceiling is a product-owner answer, not a `D-n`.** It chooses one of the remedies #721's own *Done when* lists, as plan 20's thirteenth note recommended, and adds no scope beyond the issue. It is recorded as #718's AC3 was, with a date and no number. No ADR, by the same note.
4. **The load-test tool needs neither an ADR nor a `D-n`**, on three conditions: it lives outside the shipped images and the product's Cargo and npm workspaces; it is not a required CI check; and its version or image is pinned (**D-62**). It is then a directory of scripts, cheap to reverse, constraining no product code, and trading no requirement ([standards/06](../../docs/standards/06.%20Architecture%20Decision%20Records.md) §1). **Recommended to the row**: k6, run from a pinned container image against the release stack, so nothing is installed on the host and no crate joins the build. Its README records the choice. **If the row wants it in the Cargo workspace or as a required check, that is an ADR question, raised in the row.**
5. **Encryption at rest is an ADR, not a `D-n`**: NFR-SEC-009 is a `must` already baselined by **D-5**, so the scope is settled and the shape is the question. The exception is the operator's-volume option (§4 row 2), which would be a scope call. *(2026-10-27: [ADR-0049](../../docs/architectures/adr/0049.%20The%20Object%20Store%20Encrypts%20Attachments%20Under%20Its%20Own%20Key,%20and%20a%20Backup%20Is%20Encrypted%20to%20a%20Key%20the%20Host%20Does%20Not%20Hold.md) chose the object store, and the NFR trace records the operator's volume as not chosen, so **no `D-n` is raised for NFR-SEC-009**.)*
6. **#756 is an ADR**: it adds a lock across four paths. *(2026-10-27: five, with #788: the three password writes and the two paths that store a token, [ADR-0048](../../docs/architectures/adr/0048.%20The%20User's%20Row%20Before%20Any%20Refresh%20Token,%20and%20a%20Password%20Write%20Revokes%20in%20Its%20Own%20Transaction.md).)* **#769's is conditional** on its criteria finding that it changes an order record 23 §5 states. *(2026-10-29: none is owed, §4 row 7.)*
7. **#731 rides above the line with #730**, one script. **#616 rides with #610.** **#699 goes with the release-process texts**, as plan 20 placed it.
8. **#756 and #758 are confirmed in Sprint 25**, which status report 25 left as the main session's placement.
9. **Rows 29 and 30 are decided before they are built**, by criteria put to the product owner. An answer that changes scope is the next `D-n`, and #765's cancel option would be a successor to ADR-0042. *(2026-11-06: row 29's four were decided. #765 is under **D-141** E, which cancels nothing, so ADR-0042 stands whole, and #764 is under **D-142**; §5's twenty-fifth state note.)* *(2026-11-07: all four have merged, #764 as #910 under D-142 and #765 as #912 under D-141 E; §5's twenty-sixth state note.)*
