  - **NFR-AVA-001 to 003** (containerized deployment, health checks, graceful shutdown) are in neither sprint. **Plan 21 adds them to Sprint 26's drill** (§3.3's outline).
  - **NFR-AVA-004's 99.5% monthly uptime cannot be measured before a release.** Its RPO, RTO and daily backups are what the restore drill measures. **Whether the uptime target is waived for `v1.0.0` or carried as an operating target with evidence after release is the product owner's call**, because **D-5** baselined it. Recommended: an operating target, stated in the Installation guide and `v1.0.0`'s notes, with no pre-release claim. **Plan 22 raises it as the next `D-n`** unless the product owner answers before. *(2026-11-10: the product owner answered before, so it is **D-148**, raised and answered that day: an operating target, named in `v1.0.0`'s release notes, and not a release gate; below, and §5's thirty-fourth state note.)*
  - **NFR-MNT-001 to 005 are held at every PR's review**, with no check before the tag. **NFR-MNT-005, API documentation, overlaps FR-API-004** (`Must`). **Plan 21's call: Sprint 26's documentation completion includes one check that every route is in the generated OpenAPI document**, which holds both; plan 22 sizes it.

~~**Owed since 2026-10-27, and this plan cannot take it.** The NFR trace (§4, T) raised it, and it is in the [Product Backlog](02.%20Product%20Backlog.md) §6.2.~~ **Raised by the NFR trace (§4, T) on 2026-10-27, and answered by the product owner on 2026-10-28.** It is in the [Product Backlog](02.%20Product%20Backlog.md) §6.1 (struck and restated 2026-10-28). **No row in §3.2 changes under any answer.** A adds a few sentences to row 3, and B and C each add a `must` row above the line.

- **D-119** (raised by the NFR trace, [posted on #779](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/779#issuecomment-6446835639)): **is NFR-SEC-010 met at the edge Caddy serves, or must the backend's connections to PostgreSQL, MinIO, ClamAV and SMTP also use TLS 1.2 or higher?** The [SRS](../../docs/requirements/srs.md) §5.4 reads *data in transit must use TLS 1.2 or higher*, a `must` baselined as written by **D-5**. It names no hop. **What the tree does today**:
  - **The edge**: Caddy serves TLS for a hostname (`KELIR_SITE_ADDRESS`, [Installation guide](../../docs/operations/01.%20Installation%20and%20Deployment.md) §7.2). Row 3 checks it once.
  - **SMTP**: STARTTLS is already required for any host but `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1` or `mailpit` (Installation guide §7.1, `KELIR_SMTP_HOST`).
  - **MinIO and ClamAV**: the backend reaches the store at `http://minio:9000` inside the compose network ([ADR-0049](../../docs/architectures/adr/0049.%20The%20Object%20Store%20Encrypts%20Attachments%20Under%20Its%20Own%20Key,%20and%20a%20Backup%20Is%20Encrypted%20to%20a%20Key%20the%20Host%20Does%20Not%20Hold.md) §1), and clamd by a container name that resolves inside that network and nowhere else (Installation guide §7.1, `KELIR_CLAMAV_HOST`). The stack issues no certificate for an internal service (ADR-0049, option A2).
  - **PostgreSQL**: it is outside the compose stack. The local deployment reaches the host's own install at `host.docker.internal:5432`, `KELIR_DB_HOST` can point it at any machine, and [SDD](../../docs/design/01.%20System%20Design%20Document.md) §13.2 sketches production on a managed database. **The backend's SQLx is built with rustls** (`runtime-tokio-rustls`), so ~~a database URL can ask for TLS without a new crate~~ **TLS to the database needs no new crate.** ~~**But a database URL cannot ask for it today**: the pool sets `.ssl_mode(PgSslMode::Prefer)` on every connection after it parses the URL (`kelir-backend/src/db.rs:51` at `335d202`). **So the hop tries TLS, falls back to plaintext silently, and never verifies the certificate, and any `sslmode` in `KELIR_DATABASE_URL` is overridden.** Only that forced `Prefer` stands in a URL's way~~ **But until row 39 merged, a database URL could not ask for it**: the pool set `.ssl_mode(PgSslMode::Prefer)` on every connection after it parsed the URL (`kelir-backend/src/db.rs:51` at `335d202`), **so the hop tried TLS, fell back to plaintext silently, never verified the certificate, and overrode any `sslmode` in `KELIR_DATABASE_URL`.** Only that forced `Prefer` stood in a URL's way, and B's and C's `sslmode` on the URL below assumed it did not (corrected 2026-10-28, from the main session's reading of the code; struck and restated 2026-10-29: row 39 merged as [#808](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/808), and `main` no longer forces `Prefer`, §5's fourth state note).
  - **Nothing in the SDD or the Installation guide says which internal hops a deployment trusts.**
  - **A: the edge only.** The SDD and the Installation guide would name the internal hops as a private network the deployment trusts, and nothing is built.
  - **B: every connection.** `sslmode=require` on the database URL, TLS to MinIO and SMTP's STARTTLS (already built). It is a row not yet sized. clamd has no TLS listener, so the scanner's hop needs a TLS proxy in front of it.
  - **C: the edge and the database.** The object store and the scanner stay on a private network, which the texts name.
  - **Recommended: C.** *(Answered C on 2026-10-28, the recommendation as written; the last bullet of this item.)*
    - **The database is the one internal hop the design already takes off the host.** SDD §13.2 puts it on a managed database, and `KELIR_DB_HOST` moves it anywhere today. Under A, every document's content would cross that network in plaintext, and NFR-SEC-010 was written for exactly that.
    - **C costs a setting, a probe and texts.** There is no new crate and no new image.
    - **MinIO and clamd are reached by container name, on one host.** B buys TLS on hops that do not leave the machine. It pays with certificates the stack does not issue and a proxy in front of clamd, in a sprint whose line already projects over 20,714 (§3.2).
    - **A is the cheapest answer, and a defensible reading.** But it meets a `must` by reading *in transit* as *at the edge*, and nothing in the tree would show that the database hop is private.
  - **Needed by row 3's close**, which records NFR-SEC-010, **and by the mid-sprint refinement under B or C**, so that the line's trigger counts their row. Until it is answered, **row 3 records that the edge meets NFR-SEC-010, not that the NFR is met.** *(Answered 2026-10-28: row 3 records NFR-SEC-010 as met under C once row 39 has merged.)*
  - **Impact, assessed by the project manager in `requirements-analyst`'s form**:
    - **Under A**, Sprint 25 adds only texts to row 3. They are a sentence each in SDD §13 and the Installation guide, naming the trusted network and what an operator who moves the database off the host must set: an unpinned text row, 40–200 (§3.1 rule 2). Row 3 then records NFR-SEC-010 as met under that reading, and Sprint 26 adds nothing beyond `v1.0.0`'s notes stating the boundary.
    - **Under B**, Sprint 25 adds an ADR and an unsized `must` row above the line. The ADR is for certificates issued, mounted and renewed for three internal services, and a TLS proxy in front of clamd. The proxy is a new image, pinned under **D-62**, and mirrored under **D-92** if its publisher's registries are closed. The row is TLS on the database, MinIO over TLS with the backend's endpoint `https`, the proxy, and a probe per hop. **By its shape it is row 2's order or more**, and against a line already 1,146–2,106 over by projection (§5's first state note; 506–986 when this impact was written) it would likely push row 15 and then #623 below the line. In Sprint 26, the release rehearsal, the restore drill and rows 1 and 4's rerun all run with the certificates in place.
    - **Under C**, Sprint 25 adds a small `must` row above the line. ~~Where the database is off the host, the URL carries `sslmode=require` or stricter. A probe against a PostgreSQL that requires TLS sees a plaintext URL refused and the configured one accepted.~~ **The connection requires TLS, at least `require`, for any host that is not loopback or `host.docker.internal`, with a setting for `verify-full` against an operator's CA, and a server without TLS is refused with a line naming the setting; a TLS-serving and a non-TLS PostgreSQL are each checked** (corrected 2026-10-28: a URL's `sslmode` ~~is overridden by the forced `Prefer` today~~ was overridden by the forced `Prefer` until row 39 merged (struck and restated 2026-10-29), so a URL alone could not carry it; [#803](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/803)'s *Done when*). The Installation guide §7 and SDD §13 say so. **About a control's size, 450–800** (§3.1 rule 1). In Sprint 26, the compatibility check covers a TLS database; nothing else changes.
    - **Under every answer**, SMTP's STARTTLS stays as built, and row 3's Caddy check is the edge's evidence. **MVP scope is unchanged**: SRS §9 names no NFR-SEC criterion.
  - **What is not claimed**: that any deployment runs its database off the host today; or that the local deployment's hop to `host.docker.internal` leaves the machine.
  - **ADRs**: under A and C, none is expected. Under B, the certificates for internal services are an ADR, written in the row's change ([standards/06](../../docs/standards/06.%20Architecture%20Decision%20Records.md) §1).
  - **Answered 2026-10-28: C, by the product owner, the recommendation as written.** No reason beyond the recommendation was given. **NFR-SEC-010 is met at the edge and the database, and the object store (MinIO) and the scanner (ClamAV) run on a private network the texts name.** SMTP's STARTTLS stays as built. **It is row 39, [#803](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/803)**, *deploy: the database hop may fall back to plaintext (NFR-SEC-010)*, with `area:deploy`, `area:db`, `priority:must` and `sprint:25`, **above the line at 450–800**, the recommendation's figure. **What it builds**, by #803's *Done when*: the connection requires TLS, at least `require`, for any host that is not loopback or `host.docker.internal`, with a setting for `verify-full` against an operator's CA; a server without TLS is refused with a line naming the setting; the local deployment keeps working; an operator's `sslmode` in the URL is no longer silently overridden; unit tests on the connect options seen red first, and a TLS-serving and a non-TLS PostgreSQL check, named in row 3's record; SDD §13 and the Installation guide §7 name the trust boundary; the SRS's NFR-SEC-010 note, which cites **D-119** since the answer, says the database half is built; and `CHANGELOG.md`, with an *Upgrade* note if a URL must change. **Row 3 then records NFR-SEC-010 as met under D-119 C.** **Not in it**: TLS to MinIO or ClamAV, internal certificates, and SMTP. *(2026-10-29: **built on its branch, `fix/database-hop-requires-tls`, and not merged**, with its probe, `deploy/staging/database-tls.probe.sh`, and its texts being added. The database half of NFR-SEC-010 is not on `main` until it merges; §5's third state note.)* *(**Merged 2026-10-29 as [#808](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/808)**, `f4ad231`, 1,482 added lines, closing #803: **the database half of NFR-SEC-010 is on `main`.** Row 3, not yet started, records the NFR as met under C when it runs, naming row 39's probe checks beside its own Caddy check; §5's fourth state note.)* **The correction above is part of the answer's record**: under A, today's hop would have stayed one that falls back to plaintext silently, not merely one on a trusted network. **What it costs** (§3.2's dated note): the line projects at about 23,400–24,270, and all three designated cuts leave even its bottom about 580 over 20,714; Sprint 26 projects at about 15,800–22,300, its top about 1,600 over, and plan 22's count still decides whether ~~**D-120**~~ ~~the next free number, **D-123** since 2026-10-28,~~ the next free number, **D-125** since 2026-10-29, is owed (struck and restated 2026-10-28; with D-122's row 2, Sprint 26 projects at about ~~16,400–22,900~~ 16,300–22,900, §3.2's last dated note; corrected 2026-10-29). **No ADR**, as the bullet above expected under C: a connection setting, with no new crate, image or certificate. **MVP scope is unchanged.**

**Raised and answered the same day, 2026-10-28, by the product owner: D-120, D-121 and D-122.** They are in the [Product Backlog](02.%20Product%20Backlog.md) §6.1. **The main session held each as an open candidate**, D-120 and D-121 from row 11 ([#799](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/799)) and D-122 from row 2's ADR. It flagged them and did not recommend, so **no recommendation is on record for any of the three**. None had been raised as a `D-n` before. ~~**The next free number is D-123.**~~ ~~**The next free number is D-125**~~ ~~**The next free number is D-137**~~ ~~**The next free number is D-138**~~ ~~**The next free number is D-139**~~ ~~**The next free number is D-140, since D-139 was raised by row 43's criteria and answered 1 the same day, 2026-10-31**~~ ~~**The next free number is D-141, since D-140 was raised by row 44's criteria and answered the same day, 2026-11-01**~~ ~~**The next free number is D-142, since D-141 was raised by row 23's post-merge trace on 2026-11-05, and is open**~~ ~~**The next free number is D-143, since row 29's criteria raised D-142 on 2026-11-06, and the product owner answered it and D-141 that day**~~ ~~**The next free number is D-147, since [#916](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/916) raised D-143 to D-146 on 2026-11-07**~~ ~~**The next free number is D-148, since §5's thirty-third state note raised D-147 on 2026-11-10, for [#941](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/941)**~~ **The next free number is D-149, since the product owner raised and answered D-148 on 2026-11-10, after §5's thirty-third state note raised D-147 that day, for [#941](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/941), after [#916](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/916) raised D-143 to D-146 on 2026-11-07**, after row 29's criteria raised D-142 on 2026-11-06 and the product owner answered it and D-141 that day (struck and restated 2026-11-10, the thirty-third and the thirty-fourth; 2026-11-08, §5's twenty-seventh state note; 2026-11-06, the twenty-fifth; struck and restated 2026-10-29: D-123 and D-124 were raised and answered, Product Backlog §6.1; 2026-10-31: D-125 to D-136 were raised on 2026-10-30, §5's seventh state note; later on 2026-10-31: row 42 raised D-137, §5's eighth state note; and later again: the mid-sprint refinement raised D-138, §5's ninth state note).

- **D-120: is a role code with an invisible character at its edge refused?** **What the tree does today**: `"\u{200B}ADMIN"` is stored as sent, because `str::trim` removes only whitespace. Row 11's AC7 pinned that, with `kelir-backend/tests/identity_roles.rs`'s `a_zero_width_character_at_the_edge_is_stored_as_sent_and_counted`.
  - **Answered 2026-10-28: yes, refused.** A role code that begins or ends with an invisible character is a 422 `INVALID_FORMAT` at `roleCode`, checked after `REQUIRED` and `TOO_LONG`. **Row 11's AC7 test changes with it.** **Not in it**: an interior invisible character, which the decision leaves alone; and the role name, username, email and display name, which are unchanged.
  - **It is row 40, [#805](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/805)**, *identity: a role code may begin or end with an invisible character*, **`could`, below the line, 250–500**, #805's figure. **No ADR**: one refusal in a validation that exists. **MVP scope is unchanged.**
- **D-121: do U+061C and the Hangul fillers count as invisible?** **Answered 2026-10-28: yes.** U+061C ARABIC LETTER MARK, U+115F, U+1160, U+3164 and U+FFA0 join `utils::text::INVISIBLE_CODE_POINTS`, so every `is_blank_text` check treats them as blank. **That is the identity module now, and the other modules once row 37 ([#797](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/797)) lands.** Built by row 40 with D-120. **No ADR**: five entries in a list that exists. **MVP scope is unchanged.**
- **D-122: do Kelir's backups take the object store?** [ADR-0049](../../docs/architectures/adr/0049.%20The%20Object%20Store%20Encrypts%20Attachments%20Under%20Its%20Own%20Key,%20and%20a%20Backup%20Is%20Encrypted%20to%20a%20Key%20the%20Host%20Does%20Not%20Hold.md) (`Draft`) §2 *Scope* left open *whether Kelir's backups take the object store at all, which is NFR-AVA-004's question*. **What the tree does today**: the timer takes the database only (`provision-ubuntu-24.sh` §6, `pg_dump`).
  - **Answered 2026-10-28: yes.** **It goes to row 2, [#778](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/778)**, whose *Done when* the main session widened [in a comment that day](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/778#issuecomment-6462911831): the bucket is copied beside each dump, on the same timer and with the same retention, encrypted to the same `age` recipients (MinIO decrypts SSE-S3 on read); the pre-deploy backup takes it too; the probe also reads a backed-up object raw; the restore brings back both halves, and an attachment downloads and matches, within NFR-AVA-004's RTO; ADR-0049 records D-122; and the Installation guide §8 says what a backup holds.
  - **What it costs**: row 2 grows by 300–750, to **1,200–2,750**, ADR-0049's figure, above the line (§3.2's last dated note). **The line projects at about 23,850–24,840, and all three designated cuts leave its bottom about 1,030 over 20,714 and its top about 2,000 over; Sprint 26 projects at about ~~16,400–22,900~~ 16,300–22,900, its top about 2,200 over**, and plan 22's count decides whether ~~**D-123**~~ **D-125** is owed (corrected 2026-10-29, §3.2's last dated note).
  - **The ADR**: D-122 is a scope decision, and its architecture half is ADR-0049's, which records it in its §2 and cites this row, as this row cites it. `doc-author` revises the ADR; it stays `Draft` until row 2's build flips it. *(Row 2 merged 2026-10-28 as [#806](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/806), with ADR-0049 `Adopted` in it; §5's third state note.)* **MVP scope is unchanged**: SRS §9 names no NFR-AVA criterion.

**Raised by this plan on 2026-10-31, and answered by the product owner on 2026-11-10: D-137 and D-138.** Row 42 raised D-137, and the mid-sprint refinement raised D-138. Both stood in the [Product Backlog](02.%20Product%20Backlog.md) §6.2 from 2026-10-31 to 2026-11-10, and both are now in §6.1. ~~**The next free number stays D-147.**~~ ~~**The next free number stayed D-147 until D-147 was raised below, and is D-148**~~ ~~**The next free number stayed D-147 until D-147 was raised below, was D-148 until D-148 was raised and answered below, and is D-149**~~ **The next free number stayed D-147 until D-147 was raised below, was D-148 until D-148 was raised and answered below, was D-149 until D-149 was raised and answered below, and is D-150** (struck and restated 2026-11-10, §5's thirty-third state note, and later that day, the thirty-fourth, and 2026-11-13, the thirty-sixth).

- **D-137: is a `calculate` that reads the whole record through `{"var":null}`, `[]` or `[null]` refused at a form's publish?** **Answered 2026-11-10: B, the recommendation as written.** A form's publish refuses the three with `EXPRESSION_PATH_COMPUTED`, **in `calculate` only**. A form already published is not re-checked. **It is row 48, [#936](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/936)**, below the line and outside the count, ~~about 150 lines~~ about 190–230 lines by [its criteria](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/936#issuecomment-6762587849), against D-137's about 150, ~~and being built~~ and merged on 2026-11-10 as [#938](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/938), 521 added lines (struck and restated that day, §5's thirty-second state note). #936 has no priority. **The product owner answered on #936 the same day that `{"var":[null, X]}` is refused too, if AC-48-4's probe shows it reads the whole record**; #822 had pinned it as accepted, and the answer is row 48's own question, so it takes no `D-n`. **The probe shows it does not**: the backend's evaluator answers `null` for it and reads nothing, so it stays accepted (§5's thirty-second state note). **No ADR**: one refusal on a walk that exists, as row 42 was. **MVP scope is unchanged.** *(§5's thirty-first state note.)*
- **D-138: does a row the mid-sprint refinement shed go to Sprint 26 before its freeze, or after `v1.0.0`?** **Answered 2026-11-10: A, against the recommendation of B. No reason was recorded with the answer.** **Rows 15 ([#565](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/565)), #623 out of row 14, and row 9 ([#758](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/758)) go to Sprint 26, before its freeze.** Rows 15 and #623 are release-gate rows, so they are built before `v1.0.0`'s tag. The sprint plan §6's rule is narrowed beyond D-118 C, for these three only and only before the freeze. **What it costs: about 2,110 lines at Sprint 24's ratios**, so Sprint 26 comes to about 6,900–10,700 without the four `should` issues the product owner placed there the same day. Those four are not yet sized. **No ADR**: it places work and decides no shape. **MVP scope is unchanged.** *(§5's thirty-first state note.)*

~~**Owed, and this plan cannot take it: D-147, raised by §5's thirty-third state note on 2026-11-10**~~ **Raised by §5's thirty-third state note on 2026-11-10, and answered by the product owner the same day: D-147**, from [#941](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/941), which row 48's `test-engineer` campaign filed. ~~It is in the [Product Backlog](02.%20Product%20Backlog.md) §6.2.~~ It stood in the [Product Backlog](02.%20Product%20Backlog.md) §6.2 on 2026-11-10 only, and is in §6.1 (struck and restated that day, §5's thirty-fourth state note). **No row above the line changes under any answer**, and the line stays measured at 30,285. ~~**The next free number is D-148.**~~ **The next free number is D-149**, with D-148 below (struck and restated the same day).

- **D-147: is a display component's `calculate` that reads the whole record through `{"var": ""}` or `{"var": [""]}` refused at a form's publish, as `{"var": null}`, `[]` and `[null]` are?** **What the tree does today**, at `30b9c1c`: on a display, `{"var": null}`, `{"var": []}` and `{"var": [null]}` in a `calculate` are refused with `EXPRESSION_PATH_COMPUTED`, by **D-137** B as row 48 built it, and `{"var": ""}` and `{"var": [""]}` publish. **Why they differ**: in a data component's `calculate`, `{"var": ""}` is `CALCULATION_CYCLE` at save (D-137's question); a display is not a node of [JFSS](../../docs/schema/JSON%20Form%20Schema.md) S12.2's graph, so `""` adds no cycle there, and `reads_whole_record` matches only `null`, `[]` and `[null]`. **AC-48-2 asks for the `null` refusal *on any role***, so a display is refused one spelling of the whole record and accepts another. **Low, with no server impact**: a display's `calculate` stores nothing. #942's `#[ignore]`d test `a_display_reading_the_whole_record_as_an_empty_path_is_refused_as_null_is` pins it.
  - **A: refuse the `""` family on a non-data component too**, with `EXPRESSION_PATH_COMPUTED`, at publish, outside an iterator's body; a form already published is not re-checked. A small engine change and its tests; the ignored test is un-ignored.
  - **B: stop refusing `null`, `[]` and `[null]` on a non-data component**, so a display accepts every spelling. It narrows D-137 B as row 48 built it, and AC-48-2's *on any role*.
  - **C: accept the difference, and document it in JFSS S12.2.** Nothing is built.
  - **Recommended: A.** It keeps *every spelling of the whole record refused on every role*, which is what D-137 B and AC-48-2 asked for, and costs a small engine change. B undoes part of an answer given the same day, for no reader's gain. C writes an inconsistency into a Final Standard.
  - **Under every answer, JFSS takes an erratum, E-8**: S12.2 today says a `var` whose operand is a string is not refused by this check, and names the whole-record refusal for `null`, `[]` and `[null]` only. JFSS is a Final Standard, errata only, so `schema-guardian` reads it at the row's gate.
  - **Where it would go**: under A or B, a row below the line and outside §3.2's count, as row 48 was, and **49 if it is built in Sprint 25**; not merged at the close, it goes where row 37 would have gone (the first note). Under C, a text change. **Its size is its criteria's**, not given here.
  - **Needed by Sprint 25's close** (§7 step 7), so that the row under A or B has a sprint; unanswered then, plan 22 carries it.
  - **What is not claimed**: that any tenant's stored form holds a display reading `""`; stored forms cannot be audited from the repository, so under A a tenant's next publish may be refused, as with #822 and #936.
  - **No ADR** ([standards/06](../../docs/standards/06.%20Architecture%20Decision%20Records.md) §2): under any answer it is one refusal on a walk that exists, or none, as D-137 was. **MVP scope is unchanged.**
  - **Answered 2026-11-10: A, by the product owner, the recommendation as written.** A display (non-data) component's `calculate` that reads the whole record through `{"var": ""}` or `{"var": [""]}` is refused at publish too, with `EXPRESSION_PATH_COMPUTED`, outside an iterator's body; a form already published is not re-checked. **JFSS takes erratum E-8**, in the row's change. **It is row 49, [#941](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/941)**, below the line and outside §3.2's count, as the bullet above placed it under A. #941 has no priority, and carries `sprint:25`. ~~**Placed in Sprint 25 and not yet built**~~ **Placed in Sprint 25, and merged there on 2026-11-12 as [#946](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/946), 634 added lines, every gate run before the merge** (struck and restated 2026-11-12, §5's thirty-fifth state note): `requirements-analyst`'s criteria are [posted on #941](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/941#issuecomment-6768850726) with this answer, AC-49-1 to AC-49-10, and size it at **200–450 lines, with a stop at 750**. ~~If it has not merged at Sprint 25's close, it goes where row 37 would have gone, to Sprint 26 before its freeze, and plan 22 orders it.~~ #941 ~~is still open~~ was closed later that day, at the product owner's word. **Rejected alternatives**: B, stop refusing `null`, `[]` and `[null]` on a non-data component; and C, accept the difference and document it in JFSS S12.2. **No ADR**, as the bullet above said. **MVP scope is unchanged.** *(§5's thirty-fourth state note.)*

**Raised and answered the same day, 2026-11-10, by the product owner: D-148.** It is in the [Product Backlog](02.%20Product%20Backlog.md) §6.1. **`requirements-analyst`'s impact note on D-118 put the question on 2026-10-26** (above), with a recommendation, and said plan 22 raises it as the next `D-n`; the [sprint plan](01.%20Sprint%20Plan.md) §6's Sprint 26 bullet says the same. **The product owner answered it before plan 22 raised it**, so it never stood in §6.2. ~~**The next free number is D-149.**~~ **The next free number is D-150**, with D-149 below (struck and restated 2026-11-13, §5's thirty-sixth state note).

- **D-148: is NFR-AVA-004's 99.5% monthly uptime, which no pre-release check can measure, waived at `v1.0.0` or carried as an operating target?** **Answered 2026-11-10: carried as an operating target, named in `v1.0.0`'s release notes**, the impact note's recommendation. **It is not waived, and it is not a release gate.** **What it does not touch**: NFR-AVA-004's RPO ≤ 24 hours, RTO ≤ 4 hours and daily automated backups, which Sprint 26's restore drill verifies, as the [Product Backlog](02.%20Product%20Backlog.md) §4's NFR table says. **Rejected alternative**: waived. **What is not claimed**: that any deployment has met the uptime figure; or that the Installation guide states it, which the recommendation named and the answer did not. **No ADR** ([standards/06](../../docs/standards/06.%20Architecture%20Decision%20Records.md) §2): it says how a target is reported, and decides no shape. **MVP scope is unchanged**: SRS §9 names no NFR-AVA criterion. *(§5's thirty-fourth state note.)*

**Raised and answered the same day, 2026-11-12: D-149.** The product owner chose [#948](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/948), row 49's follow-up, as row 50, and `requirements-analyst` drafted the question it raised. **The product owner answered it the same day**, so it never stood in §6.2. It is in the [Product Backlog](02.%20Product%20Backlog.md) §6.1. **No row above the line changes under any answer**, and the line stays measured at 30,285. **The next free number is D-150.**

- **D-149: are a display's and a layout's `calculate` and `conditional.logic` held to the [Calculation Rule Registry](../../docs/schema/JFSS%20Calculation%20Rule%20Registry.md) and the `conditional` floor, as a data field's are?** **What the tree did**, at `4e88d7f`: `check_operator_sets` walked data components only, so a save of `{"Var": ""}` on a display returned 201. **The gap was a regression**: [#344](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/344) (`4312a81`, 2026-09-05) moved the operator walk into the data-only `ScopeCatalogue`, and the walk it replaced read every role.
  - **A: a display's `calculate` meets the registry, and every non-data role's `conditional.logic` meets the floor**, the registry's §2.5, at save and at publish.
  - **B: `conditional` only.**
  - **C: leave it, and document it.**
  - **D: A, plus a display tier that admits `cat`.**
  - **Recommended: A.**
  - **Answered 2026-11-12: A, by the product owner, the recommendation, with `priority:should`.** A display's `calculate` meets the registry, and every non-data role's `conditional.logic` meets the `conditional` floor, at save and at publish. **It is row 50, [#948](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/948)**, below the line and outside §3.2's count, with `priority:should` and `sprint:25`, both applied by the main session that day. **The answer and `requirements-analyst`'s criteria are [posted on #948](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/948#issuecomment-6825241034)**, AC-50-1 to AC-50-12, and size it at **250–500 lines, with a stop at 750**. **Placed in Sprint 25, and merged there on 2026-11-13 as [#950](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/pull/950)**, 1,001 added lines, past the stop by the product owner's choice, every gate run before the merge. The registry goes to 1.7.2, a patch, and JFSS takes no erratum. #948 was closed by the main session after the merge. **Rejected alternatives**: B, `conditional` only; C, leave it and document it; and D, A with a display tier that admits `cat`. **What is not claimed**: that a tenant's stored forms hold no display or layout whose expressions the check now refuses; they cannot be audited from the repository, so a tenant's next save or publish may be refused, as with #822, #936 and #941. **[#947](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/947) stays separate**, unprioritised and unplaced. **No ADR** ([standards/06](../../docs/standards/06.%20Architecture%20Decision%20Records.md) §2): it restores a check to roles a walk skipped, and decides no new shape. **MVP scope is unchanged.** *(§5's thirty-sixth state note.)*

|---|---|---|---|---|
| **F1**, [#777](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/777) | test: no load test shows list and detail views meet their p95 targets (NFR-PERF-001) | `area:testing`, `priority:should`, `sprint:25` | Row 1 | A scenario set signs in 50 users once each and drives the requests a list view and a detail view make, as the NFR trace's criteria define them, against the release stack in its own compose project with 10,000 documents in a throwaway tenant. One Chromium measurement times each view under that load. The P95 of each is recorded on this issue and in ~~SDD §13~~ a new SDD §13.4 (corrected 2026-10-27 by the NFR trace), with the machine named, against 1 s for a list and 2 s for a detail. A breach is filed as its own issue. The tool runs from a pinned version, outside the product's images and workspaces, and is not a required check. A README says how to run it |
| **F2**, [#778](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/778) | deploy: attachments and backups are not encrypted at rest (NFR-SEC-009) | `area:deploy`, `priority:must`, `sprint:25` | Row 2 | An ADR, adopted, records how attachments and backups are encrypted at rest and what it rejected. The shipped compose definitions and the backup timer do it. A probe reads one stored object and one backup archive raw, finds each encrypted, and is seen red with encryption off. A backup restores through the documented path. The Installation guide says what an operator must keep, and what is lost without it. *(Widened 2026-10-28 by **D-122**, [in a comment on #778](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/778#issuecomment-6462911831): the bucket is copied beside each dump, on the same timer and with the same retention, encrypted to the same `age` recipients; the pre-deploy backup takes it too; the probe also reads a backed-up object raw; the restore brings back both halves, an attachment downloads and matches, within NFR-AVA-004's RTO; ADR-0049 records D-122; and the Installation guide §8 says what a backup holds.)* |
| **F3**, [#779](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/779) | test: no record shows NFR-SEC-001 to 010 met before 1.0 | `area:testing`, `priority:must`, `sprint:25` | Row 3 | Each of NFR-SEC-001 to 010 is traced to the tests that hold it, or to a finding filed. ~~Both lock files are~~ The two shipped lock files, `kelir-backend/Cargo.lock` and `kelir-frontend/package-lock.json`, are audited once for known vulnerabilities, and each finding is filed. The Caddy configuration refuses TLS below 1.2 on a hostname, checked once. A target no test holds, ~~such as NFR-SEC-008's 5 failures and 15 minutes~~ the Argon2 `m`, `t` and `p`, a walk of the routes with no token, and a log captured on a request's path, gains a pin seen red. *(Corrected 2026-10-27 by the NFR trace: NFR-SEC-008 is held by `auth_lockout.rs`, and the tree has six lock files. | **F4**, [#780](https://github.com/sujanto-gaws/kelir/issues/780) | deploy: nothing shows the backend runs as two instances (NFR-SCA-001) | `area:deploy`, `priority:should`, `sprint:25` | Row 4 | A compose overlay runs two backends behind Caddy, and the browser suite and F1's smoke scenario pass against it. Every piece of per-process state is named, and the Installation guide says what each does with two instances, the rate limiter among them. NFR-SCA-002 to 004 are traced to where the tree meets them |
